请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
宏观研究
宏观点评
内外需延续分化格局
——10月宏观数据点评
事件：2026 年 11 月 16日，国家统计局发布 10 月份国内宏观经济数据。10
月份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，社会消费品零售总额同比增
长 0.4%；1—10 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 8.1%，房地产
开发投资同比下降 21.6%。
核心观点：
 10 月国内经济内外需分化格局延续，“供强需弱”特征进一步显现。各
口径表现情况看，出口高增与新兴产业快速发展继续对工业生产形成有
力支撑，10 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，高技术制造业、装备
制造业增加值分别增长 17.6%和 10.6%；但内需端进一步走弱，社零受
假期错位影响增速再度放缓，10 月同比增长 0.4%、较上月回落 0.7 个百
分点，1—10 月固定资产投资同比下降 8.1%，房地产开发投资下降
21.6%，降幅较上月分别扩大 0.9和 1.0个百分点。

 往后看，伴随各项稳增长政策落地，包括新型政策性金融工具、专项债
等资金加快形成实物工作量，以及房地产因城施策与城市更新持续推进
等，后期国内经济增长动能有望边际改善。但政策力度与节奏、外需持
续性及海外货币政策变化仍是主要不确定性来源。
 风险提示：1、政策进度不及预期；2、出口回落超预期；3、美联储政策
紧缩超预期。
2026 年 11 月 18 日

### Page 3

1、外需与新动能继续对工业生产形成支撑
10 月份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长
4.8%，增速较上月的 5.5%回落 0.7 个百分点，低于
市场一致预期的增长5.3%，1—10月累计增长5.3%。
10 月外需与新动能继续对工业生产形成支撑。
以美元计当月国内出口同比增长35.2%，受此带动规
模以上工业企业出口交货值同比名义增长18.6%，外
需对工业生产的拉动作用持续显现。另一方面，10
月份高技术制造业增加值同比增长17.6%，装备制造
业增长 10.6%，分别快于全部规模以上工业 12.8 和
5.8个百分点；3D打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器
人产量同比分别增长85.9%、30.0%和26.0%，新能源
汽车产量增长17.1%，新动能产业继续为工业生产注
入强劲动能。
图表 1 规模以上工业增加值增速（%） 图表 2 规上工业企业出口交货值增速（%）
数据来源：同花顺 iFinD、银泰证券研究所

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
分三大门类看，10 月采矿业增加值同比增长
0.7%，制造业增长 5.4%，电力、热力、燃气及水生
产和供应业增长4.4%，较上月分别放缓0.6、0.6和
1.2个百分点。
分行业看，10 月份 41 个大类行业中有 28 个行
业增加值保持同比增长，增长面为68.3%。其中装备
与电子相关行业景气度整体领先，计算机、通信和其
他电子设备制造业增长19.4%，专用设备制造业增长
11.5%，铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造
业增长9.2%，通用设备制造业增长8.5%，电气机械
和器材制造业增长8.1%，汽车制造业增长 0.9%。传
统周期行业多数继续承压，有色金属冶炼和压延加工
业下降4.4%，煤炭开采和洗选业下降2.9%，非金属
矿物制品业下降 2.3%，黑色金属冶炼和压延加工业
下降1.7%，化学原料和化学制品制造业仅增长1.3%。
图表 3 工业三大门类增速（%） 图表 4 10月主要行业增加值同比（%）
数据来源：同花顺 iFinD、银泰证券研究所

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
2、假期错位影响社零增速再度放缓
10月份，社会消费品零售总额同比增长0.4%，
增速较上月的1.1%回落0.7个百分点，低于市场一
致预期的0.8%，1—10月累计增长1.0%。当月社零
增速放缓，中秋假期错位带来明显扰动，但国内消费
需求偏弱同样是重要原因。
按消费类型分，10月商品零售额同比增长0.3%
（上月为+1.0%），餐饮增长0.7%（上月为+2.1%），
增速分别放缓0.7 和1.4 个百分点。10月份限额以
上单位消费品零售额同比下降4.9%，降幅较上月扩
大2.8个百分点，自4 月以来持续处于负增长区间。
主要商品类别中，汽车类同比下降21.4%（上月为
-21.2%），继续对社零整体形成拖累；此外通讯器材
类同比增长6.5%（上月为31.1%），增速较上月明显
放缓，家用电器和音像器材类增长2.5%（上月为
1.9%）边际有所改善。其它商品类别中，烟酒类增长
2.5%（上月为13.8%），饮料类增长0.3%（上月为

5.0%），文化办公用品类增长4.1%（上月为7.9%），
中西药品类增长3.7%（上月为5.0%），化妆品类增
长2.8%（上月为5.4%），增速较上月均有不同程度
的放缓。

3、固投延续调整，新动能投资较快增长
1—10月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同
比下降 8.1%，降幅较 1—9 月的-7.2%扩大 0.9个百分

### Page 7

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
点，低于市场一致预期的-6.8。民间固定资产投资同
比下降 11.4%，降幅继续扩大。
分领域看，基础设施投资同比下降 5.1%，降幅
较 1—9月扩大 0.9个百分点；制造业投资下降 2.9%，
降幅扩大 0.8个百分点。
结构上看，投资向新动能集中的特征进一步强
化。1—10 月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，占
全部投资比重 16.2%，拉动全部投资增长 1.3个百分
点；设备工器具购置投资增长 8.9%，占比 20.3%、
比上年同期提高 3.2个百分点，拉动全部投资增长 1.5
个百分点。高技术产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信
息服务业投资增长 20.5%、航空、航天器及设备制造
业增长 10.2%、电子及通信设备制造业增长 6.6%。
基建投资落地进程偏慢，1—10月电力、热力、
燃气及水生产和供应业投资下降 7.7%（1—9 月为
-6.8%），交通运输、仓储和邮政业增长 0.7%（1—9
月为+1.9%），水利、环境和公共设施管理业下降

14.4%（1—9月为-12.8%），投资增速较上月均有不
同程度的放缓。

4、房地产延续筑底，开发投资降幅扩大
1—10 月份，全国房地产开发投资同比下降
21.6%，降幅较1—9 月的-20.6%扩大1.0个百分点。
房屋施工面积下降13.0%，新开工面积下降24.4%，
竣工面积下降 26.6%，开发端各环节仍全面处于低

位。
销售端看，1—10 月新建商品房销售面积同比
下降13.7%，降幅较1—9月扩大 0.5个百分点；销
售额下降14.3%，降幅扩大0.4个百分点。
相对积极的是，二手房交易持续改善。1—10
月全国二手房交易网签面积 67971 万平方米、同比
增长11.4%，增速较1—9月加快 0.7个百分点。
房价方面，10月份一线城市新建商品住宅销售
价格环比由上月上涨0.1%转为持平，其中上海、深
圳分别上涨0.2%和0.1%，广州持平、北京下降0.1%；
二线城市环比由持平转为下降0.1%，三线城市下降
0.2%、降幅与上月相同；70个大中城市中新建商品
住宅价格环比上涨城市14个，比上月减少3 个。
二手住宅方面，一线城市环比上涨0.2%、涨幅
与上月持平（上海+0.5%、广州+0.2%、深圳+0.1%、
北京持平），二线、三线城市均下降0.3%；二手住
宅价格环比上涨城市7 个，比上月减少2 个。
同比看，一二三线城市新建商品住宅价格降幅

分别收窄0.4、0.4和0.3个百分点，二手住宅价格
降幅分别收窄1.1、0.4和0.4个百分点，一线城市
价格企稳趋势继续得到确认，但全国层面地产仍处
于筑底阶段。

Document 005: 10月经济数据点评：以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动

### Page 1

证券投资咨询业务证书编号：S1300516090001

10 月经济数据点评

以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动   
10 月经济数据整体偏弱，表现较好的仅有高技术产业工业增加值同比增速；
我们认为国庆长假前后居民休假，可能对 10 月工作日时间的影响较明显，进
而导致经济数据表现偏弱。 
◼ 10 月工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，从行业分类来看，1-10 月采矿业工业增
加值累计同比增速 1.9%，制造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用事业累计同比
增速 5.3%，高技术产业累计同比增速 14.6%。 
◼ 10 月社零同比增长 0.4%，其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长
2.5%，1-10 月服务消费累计同比增长 4.9%。 
◼ 1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速下降 8.1%，民间固定资产投资累计同
比下降 11.4%，扣除房地产开发投资，1-10 月全国固定资产投资下降 5.1%，
民间投资下降 7.8%。分主要行业看，制造业投资 1-10 月累计同比下降
2.9%，基建投资累计同比下降 7.3%，地产投资累计同比下降 21.6%。 
◼ 1-10 月商品房销售面积同比下降 13.7%，商品房销售额累计同比下降
14.3%。10 月，70 个大中城市新建商品住宅价格指数同比下降 2.7%，较
9 月上升 0.3 个百分点，二手住宅价格指数下降 4.4%，较 9 月上升 0.4 个
百分点。 
◼ 10 月经济数据偏弱。工业增加值同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且
低于万得一致预期，社零同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得
一致预期，1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速较 1-9 月下行 0.9 个百分点，
且低于万得一致预期。从细项看，10 月经济数据中表现较好的是工业增
加值中的高技术产业，以及依托外需的出口交货值，此前经济数据中表
现较好的部分，如固定资产投资中的设备购置累计同比增速、社零中的
通信工具当月同比增速，在 10 月也都出现了增速下降的情况。另一方面，
此前偏弱的数据尚未出现好转迹象，包括房地产固定资产投资增速和商
品房销售数据同比增速仍在下降，基建投资增速仍在下滑等等。考虑到
今年的国庆和中秋假期相邻，部分居民选择利用年假增加国庆前后出行
时间，我们认为 10 月经济数据偏弱，一定程度上可能有假期安排对工作
日天数缩短的影响。 
◼ 关注 2027 年经济增长目标设定。12 月政治局会议时间一般在上旬，这就
意味着 10 月经济数据对于定调 2027 年经济工作的影响较大。从 10 月数
据的特点来看，我们认为较为确定的几个方向：一是三驾马车中净出口
仍将是 2027 年经济增长的重要拉动力，二是消费偏弱的状态大概率将延
续，三是基建投资增速仍较大程度上依赖财政支出，短期内的亮点可能
是“六张网”，四是房地产行业即便有政策支持，整体或仍在寻底的过
程中，连带房地产产业链上下游中期内都将处于寻底过程，五是高技术
产业或将保持较好增速，六是国内通胀压力整体处于偏低水平，七是适
度宽松的货币政策环境和积极的财政政策大概率都将延续。我们认为
2027 年可能需要关注供给端的持续稳健经营：一方面，美国对 AI 科技发
展的监管政策前景存在不确定性，对于当前全球范围内 AI 带动的经济增
长结构或产生一定影响，进而影响我国外需，另一方面，全球范围内能
源价格上涨、国内 PPI 同比高增和人民币的升值趋势，都可能推升供给端
成本，进而影响企业生产经营。我们认为不排除 2027 年经济增长目标设
定为 2026 年增长目标区间的下限。 
◼ 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 2

工业增加值同比增速回落 
10 月工业增加值同比增长 4.8%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，较去年同期下降 0.1 个百分点，低于万
得一致预期同比增速 5.32%。

图表 1. 工业增加值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券

从环比增速来看，10 月工业增加值环比上升 0.21%，与 2016 年-2019 年期间同期平均水平 0.47%相
比偏低,略低于去年同期的环比增速 0.22%，10 月工业增加值环比较 9 月下降,趋势上符合季节性规律，
但受 9 月环比基数偏高影响，10 月环比增速回落幅度较大。 
从行业分类来看，1-10 月采矿业工业增加值累计同比增速 1.9%，制造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用
事业累计同比增速 5.3%，高技术产业累计同比增速 14.6%。从企业类型来看，1-10 月国企工业增加
值同比增速 3.6%，股份制企业同比增速 5.7%，外企工业增加值同比增速 3.2%，私企工业增加值同
比增速 4.2%。与 1-9 月相比，1-10 月累计同比增速上升的仅有高技术产业,下降的是国企、采矿业、
公共事业、股份制企业和私企。10 月当月工业增加值同比增速相较 9 月，各行业和企业类型中工业
增加值同比增速上升的也仅有高技术产业，下降幅度超过 1.0 个百分点的是国企和公共事业。 
1-10 月，装备制造业增加值同比增长 10.6%，高技术制造业增加值增长 17.6%，分别快于全部规模
以上工业增加值 5.8 和 12.8 个百分点；分产品看，3D 打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器人产品产量
同比分别增长 85.9%、30.0%、26.0%。

### Page 3

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 3

10 月，采矿业和大宗周期品加工业当月工业增加值同比增速偏弱，中游设备制造业工业增加值表现
相对较好。 
采矿业方面，1-10 月煤炭开采和洗选业增加值累计同比下降 0.1%，石油和天然气开采业增加值累计
同比增长 6.1%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速小幅上升的是煤炭开采，小幅下降的是油气开采，
但煤炭开采行业 10 月当月工业增加值同比仍在负增长区间。 
消费类制造业方面，1-10 月农副食品加工业增加值累计同比增长 4.5%，纺织业增加值累计同比增长
3.0%，医药制造业增加值累计同比增长 5.3%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速上升的是医药制造
业，降幅超过 1.0 个百分点的是纺织业。 
大宗周期品加工业方面，1-10 月化学原料及化学制品制造业增加值累计同比增长 3.3%，非金属矿物
制品业增加值累计同比下降 2.9%，黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比增长 1.4%，有色金属
冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比下降 1.9%；从当月同比增速来看，较 9 月增速上升的有色金属和
非金属矿物，增速升幅较小，但较 9 月增速下降的化学原料和黑色金属降幅均超过 1.0 个百分点。 
中游设备制造业方面，1-10 月通用设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 8.2%，专用设备制造业增加值累
计同比增长 9.7%，汽车制造业增加值累计同比增长 6.5%，电气机械及器材制造业增加值累计同比增
长 7.2%，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 16.2%；从当月同比增速来看，
较 9 月增速上升的仅有计算机通信，且升幅达到 3.0 个百分点，下降的是汽车制造业和专用设备制
造业，其中汽车制造业较 9 月降幅达到 5.8 个百分点，10 月当月工业增加值同比增速仅为 0.9%。

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 4

10 月外需延续较强走势，出口交货值同比继续大幅上升，产销率较去年同期小幅上升。 
10 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 96.5%，较 9 月下降 0.5 个百分点，较去年同期上升 0.1 个百分点，
当月同比增速下降 0.4%；1-10 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 96.4%，较去年同期上升 0.4 个百分点，
累计同比增速持平。 
10 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 18.6%，较 9 月上升 1.6 个百分点，1-10 月规模以上工
业企业出口交货值累计同比增长 11.6%，较 1-9 月上升 0.9 个百分点。

图表 8. 产销率当月值和累计同比增速  图表 9. 出口交货值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

社零增速再度回落 
10 月社零同比增长 0.4%，较 9 月下降0.7 个百分点，其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长2.5%，
较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点；1-10 月社零累计同比增长 1.0%，其中，服务消费累计同比增长 4.9%，较
1-9 月下降 0.1 个百分点。 
10 月社零环比增速增长 0.02%，较去年同期环比增速下降 0.09 个百分点，较历史同期平均水平明显
偏低。

图表 11. 汽车以外社零和服务消费增速  图表 12. 社零环比增速的历史波动

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

1-10 月网上商品和服务零售额累计同比增长 3.7%，增速较 1-9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，其中实物商品
网上零售额累计同比增长 2.9%，增速较 1-9 月下降 0.9 个百分点。10 月网上零售额累计占比为 40.8%，
较 2026 年同期上升 9.7 个百分点，较 1-9 月下降 0.2 个百分点。 
10 月商品消费同比增长 0.3%，较 9 月下降 0.7 个百分点，限额以上商品消费同比下降 5.1%，较 9
月下降 2.6 个百分点；10 月餐饮消费同比增长 0.7%，较 9 月下降 1.4 个百分点，限额以上餐饮消费
同比下降 2.9%，较 9 月下降 4.9 个百分点。1-10 月，在服务零售额中，通讯信息服务类、旅游咨询
租赁服务类、文体休闲服务类零售额较快增长。 
1-10 月限额以上企业消费品零售总额累计同比下降 1.9%，限额以上企业商品消费累计同比下降 2.1%,
弱于餐饮消费累计同比增速 0.9%。

图表 13. 网上零售总额的占比和增速  图表 14. 限额以上企业社零增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

10 月受价格因素影响，石油制品消费增速有所上升，房地产后周期消费依然偏弱，国庆假期时间较
长，一定程度上拖累了居民消费增速表现。 
从产品分类来看，10 月限额以上企业商品零售总额增速较高的细分行业包括通讯器材类（6.5%）、
石油及制品类（4.4%）和文化办公用品类（4.1%），增速较低的细分行业包括金银珠宝类（-28.7%）、
汽车类（-21.4%）和建筑及装潢材料类（-11.2%）；与 9 月相比，增速上升的有石油及制品类、体
育娱乐用品类、家用电器和音像器材类和家具，增速下降较多的有通讯器材类、金银珠宝类和烟酒
类等。

1-10 月累计同比增速较高的品类有通讯器材类、烟酒类和粮油食品类等，累计同比增速较低的品类
有汽车类、建筑及装潢材料类和金银珠宝类等；累计同比增速较 1-9 月上升的仅有石油及制品类、
家用电器和音像器材类、体育娱乐用品类和家具，累计同比增速较 1-9 月下降较多的有金银珠宝类、
通讯器材类和烟酒类等。

固定资产投资累计同比增速降幅再度加大 
1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速下降 8.1%，较 1-9 月下降 0.9 个百分点，1-10 月民间固定资产投
资累计同比下降 11.4%，较 1-9 月下降 1.0 个百分点。扣除房地产开发投资，1-10 月全国固定资产投
资下降 5.1%，民间投资下降 7.8%。10 月固定资产投资环比下降 2.93%。

图表 17. 固定资产投资累计同比和民间固定资产投资累计同比增速

计同比增长 8.9%，其他费用累计同比下降 13.2%；与 1-9 月相比，增速分别下降 1.0 个百分点、下
降 0.8 个百分点和下降 1.1 个百分点。

### Page 7

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 7

图表 18. 固定资产投资环比增速的季节规律  图表 19. 固投各项构成分类的累计同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

10 月制造业、基建和房地产当月投资增速较 9 月均出现回落。 
从产业分类来看，第一产业累计同比下降 6.1%，第二产业累计同比下降 3.6%，第三产业累计同比下
降 10.9%；从地区来看，东部累计同比下降 11.5%，中部下降 12.9%，西部下降 9.2%，东北下降 25.9%；
从企业类型来看，内资企业累计同比下降 7.9%，国有控股同比下降 4.0%，外商投资下降 6.6%。 
分主要行业看，制造业投资 1-10 月累计同比下降 2.9%，基建投资累计同比下降 7.3%，地产投资累
计同比下降 21.6%。从我们测算的当月同比增速看，10 月制造业投资同比增速下降 9.3%，较 9 月下
降 8.4 个百分点，基建投资同比增速下降 17.4%，较 9 月下降 6.9 个百分点，房地产投资同比增速下
降 34.2%，较 9 月下降 7.0 个百分点。 
1-10 月固投增速在正增长区间的一级分类行业是采矿业（2.5%）和交运仓储（0.7%），固投增速较
低的行业包括文体娱（-18.5%）、教育（-15.7%）和水利环境（-14.4%）。制造业行业分类下，固
投增速较高的细分行业包括铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业（14.1%）、计算机、通信
和其他电子设备制造业（9.9%）和纺织业（2.8%），固投增速较低的行业包括食品制造业（-10.7%）、
金属制品业（-9.9%）和医药制造业（-9.2%）。 
与 1-9 月相比，一级分类行业中固投增速降幅较小的行业是卫生社会、教育和采矿业，固投增速下
降较多的行业是农林牧渔业、水利环境和文体娱。制造业行业分类下，固投增速上行的行业仅有计
算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业、有色金属冶炼及压延加工业和金属制品业，固投增速下行较多
的行业是通用设备制造业、铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业和汽车制造业。 
据统计局解读，1-10 月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.2%，高技术产业投资同比增长 4.0%，其中信
息服务业，航空、航天器及设备制造业，电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.5%、10.2%、6.6%。

### Page 10

2026 年 11 月 16 日 10 月经济数据点评 10

结论：以稳定的生产端应对需求端波动 
10 月经济数据偏弱。工业增加值同比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期，社零同
比增速较 9 月下行 0.7 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期，1-10 月固定资产投资累计同比增速较 1-9
月下行 0.9 个百分点，且低于万得一致预期。从细项看，10 月经济数据中表现较好的是工业增加值
中的高技术产业，以及依托外需的出口交货值，此前经济数据中表现较好的部分，如固定资产投资
中的设备购置累计同比增速、社零中的通信工具当月同比增速，在 10 月也都出现了增速下降的情况。
另一方面，此前偏弱的数据尚未出现好转迹象，包括房地产固定资产投资增速和商品房销售数据同
比增速仍在下降，基建投资增速仍在下滑等等。考虑到今年的国庆和中秋假期相邻，部分居民选择
利用年假增加国庆前后出行时间，我们认为 10 月经济数据偏弱，一定程度上可能有假期安排对工作
日天数缩短的影响。 
关注 2027 年经济增长目标设定。12 月政治局会议时间一般在上旬，这就意味着 10 月经济数据对于
定调 2027 年经济工作的影响较大。从 10 月数据的特点来看，我们认为较为确定的几个方向：一是
三驾马车中净出口仍将是 2027 年经济增长的重要拉动力，二是消费偏弱的状态大概率将延续，三是
基建投资增速仍较大程度上依赖财政支出，短期内的亮点可能是“六张网”，四是房地产行业即便有
政策支持，整体或仍在寻底的过程中，连带房地产产业链上下游中期内都将处于寻底过程，五是高
技术产业或将保持较好增速，六是国内通胀压力整体处于偏低水平，七是适度宽松的货币政策环境
和积极的财政政策大概率都将延续。我们认为 2027 年可能需要关注供给端的持续稳健经营：一方面，
美国对 AI 科技发展的监管政策前景存在不确定性，对于当前全球范围内 AI 带动的经济增长结构或
产生一定影响，进而影响我国外需，另一方面，全球范围内能源价格上涨、国内 PPI 同比高增和人
民币的升值趋势，都可能推升供给端成本，进而影响企业生产经营。我们认为不排除 2027 年经济增
长目标设定为 2026 年增长目标区间的下限。 
风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准： 
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

The bank
for a changing

3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026EDITORIAL
2026 HAS EXPOSED NEW RISKS FOR THE EUROPEAN GAS MARKET
1 C. Grekou, V. Mignon, L. Ragot, 2026, ‘From Russian gas to the global LNG market: The European Union’s new vulnerability’, CEPII, Policy Brief.
2 ACER, 2026, ‘Analysis of the European LNG market development. Monitoring report’.
3 These contracts allow the buyer to choose, or change, the delivery point for the shipment. Recent US contracts (Free On Board and flexible destination) generally

feature these characteristics.
4 IEA, 2026, ‘Global Gas Security Review & Gas Market Report Q4-2026’.
Europe’s vulnerability to the gas market has changed in recent years. Whilst its reliance on imports has remained stable,
its gas supply has been more vulnerable to global market conditions since 2022, due to a shift in the composition of its
imports. Furthermore, in 2026, the combination of high geopolitical risk in one of the main production regions and the

pressure of climate change on European consumers has created an unprecedented situation, leading to price pressures
and significant uncertainty regarding the short-term outlook. Even if they ease, tensions in the European gas market could
persist beyond the winter, largely due to the need to refill inventories.
Whilst Europe is not heavily reliant on gas imports from the Middle
East, it has had to contend with a fresh surge in prices since the out -

break of war in Iran last February, a rise that has, in part, been passed
on to electricity prices this summer. Furthermore, new sources of
vulnerability, particularly those linked to climate change, are at play.
These call for caution regarding the short-term outlook for the Euro -
pean gas market.
EUROPE’S GROWING EXPOSURE TO MARKET VOLATILITY
Unlike the 2022 energy crisis, which hit Europe directly, the war in
Iran is indirectly affecting the European gas market. 80% of gas exports

from the Middle East are destined for Asia. However, the growing inter-
connection between the two main gas-importing regions — Asia and
Europe — has resulted in the crisis spreading to Europe. Since 2022,
the transmission of shocks to liquefied natural gas (LNG) benchmark
prices on the European and Asian markets (TTF and JKM) has increased
significantly, in both directions1.
Despite a fall of around 20% in European gas consumption since 2022,
the replacement of around 60% of Russian pipeline imports with pur -

chases of liquefied natural gas (see Chart 1) has kept Europe’s vulne -
rability at a high level. Given developments in the LNG market —
growing interconnection between European and Asian markets and
the importance of spot contracts (around one-third of European supply
contracts2) — Europe finds itself, through no fault of its own, increa -
singly exposed to gas price volatility.
Furthermore, the increasing autonomy and flexibility of the global
LNG market may exacerbate this volatility. The higher proportion of

LNG sales contracts with flexible destinations3 (rising from 29% to 51%
of the total between 2016 and 2025), linked to the massive influx of
US LNG onto the market, together with the reduction in the share of
contracts indexed to oil prices (from 70% to 55% over the same period),
have made price formation on the LNG market more autonomous, the-
reby increasing the opportunities for arbitrage and the flexibility of this
market.
MAJOR DISRUPTIONS IN THE LNG MARKET HAVE CONSTRAINED THE REFIL-

LING OF EUROPEAN INVENTORIES
The 85% reduction in LNG exports from Qatar and the UAE in Q2 and Q3
2026 was offset by two-thirds through, on the one hand, an increase
in exports from other producing countries (primarily the United States)
and, on the other hand, a fall in demand in Asia and Europe (which
is forecast to reach, respectively, 1.5% and 3% year-on-year in 2026,
according to IEA forecasts4). Owing to the damage caused by the war to
infrastructure (17% of Qatar’s production capacity is out of service) and

logistical constraints specific to the LNG market (unavailability of LNG
carriers), the LNG market has benefited only marginally from the re -
sumption of flows through the Strait of Hormuz since July. Against this
backdrop of tensions in the gas market, high prices in Europe (up by
around 174% since the outbreak of the conflict) have significantly
slowed the refilling of European stocks. They stood at 75% of full capa-
city at the end of October, whilst the official target is 80% (see Chart 2).

OVER THE WINTER, DIVERGENT FACTORS COULD STABILISE PRICES AT A
HIGH LEVEL
Gas stock levels that are below the target set by the European Commis-
sion do not increase the risk of a shortage on the European continent.
Nevertheless, Europe’s vulnerability towards the end of winter, in the
event of a cold snap, would force it to buy on the market at high prices.
SOURCE: BRUEGEL, BNP PARIBAS

### Page 2

economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.41 / 9 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Furthermore, from a technical perspective, exposure to the conse -
quences of a cold snap at the end of winter is all the more pronounced
as the rate at which gas can be withdrawn from storage (the ease with
which a country can access its supplies) decreases (due to lower stock
levels in storage facilities).
Apart from stock levels at the start of winter, three factors will deter -

mine the situation at the end of the season, a key period of vulnera -
bility:
1/ the resumption of exports from the Gulf, a region that is essential for
restoring market balance in the short term;
2/ weather conditions during the winter;
3/ renewable energy production.
According to the IEA, in the worst-case scenario (with exports from the
Middle East only resuming from the end of the first quarter of 2027, a
cold winter and low renewable energy production), stocks would stand

at 7% of total capacity by the end of winter, compared with 33% in
the best-case scenario. Such levels remain significantly below the ave-
rage for the last five years at this time of year: 80% in the worst-case
scenario and 18% in the best-case scenario. This would underpin res -
tocking demand over the next two quarters, and therefore keep prices
at a fairly high level. For the time being, a significant recovery in LNG
exports from the Gulf remains highly uncertain, even though indirect

talks between the warring parties have resumed. On a more positive
note, the current development of a very powerful El Niño event should,
in principle, be associated with relatively mild temperatures during the
winter in western Europe. According to the futures markets, European
gas prices are not expected to return to their pre-crisis levels until
2028 (see Chart 3).
ARE WE HEADING FOR A SUMMER PEAK IN GAS DEMAND?
In addition to these factors, which are likely to prolong market tensions

during the first half of 2027, the summer period could also be cha -
racterised by increased demand for gas. During the summer of 2026,
5 In early October 2026, the water levels in French reservoirs reached a ten-year low, standing at 23% below seasonal averages.
6 RTE data.
7 M. Mueller, P. Devaux, October 2026, ‘What lessons can we learn to keep European industry competitive from the measures taken by Germany, as the cost of electricity
continues to turn the screw?’, EcoInsight.

8 Oxford Institute for Energy Studies, October 2026, Quarterly Gas Market Review.
weather conditions hampered the generation of certain forms of re -
newable energy (heatwaves and droughts restricting electricity gene -
ration from hydroelectric5 and wind power sources). They also disrup-
ted nuclear power generation (in France, nuclear electricity generation
fell by around 10% year-on-year between August and October6). Along-
side these constraints on carbon-free electricity generation, heatwaves

are driving up demand for electricity, particularly for air conditioning.
Against this backdrop, as a source of flexibility within the electricity
mix, gas remains a key determinant of electricity prices in Europe 7.
In Q3 2026, whilst the decline in European gas consumption mainly
affected industry, the residential sector and the commercial sector 8,
consumption by gas-fired power stations was 9% higher than in 2025.
STRENGTHENING EUROPEAN PROGRAMMES

In this context, and despite the progress made in decarbonising the
European electricity mix, high gas prices have led to a rise in whole -
sale electricity prices in most European countries. Between July and
September 2026, these prices were on average 54% higher than in the
same period a year earlier.
Against this backdrop of widespread rises in energy prices, European go-
vernments have primarily introduced measures to support households’
purchasing power when buying fuel. Measures relating to gas supply

remain fairly limited. The European Commission has extended the tar-
get for refilling European stocks to 80% (instead of 90%) for 2026. It has
also urged Member States to extend the stock-refill season in order
to smooth out purchase prices. Furthermore, the measures taken in
2022 under the RePower EU programme have been maintained: redu -
cing dependence on Russian gas with the aim of a complete phase-out
(pipeline and LNG) by the end of 2027; and pooling gas purchases at

European level. The ‘decarbonisation and electrification’ component of
this programme was strengthened in April 2026 by the Accelerate EU
programme, notably through measures to accelerate the electrification
of demand and the development of electricity grid infrastructure at
European level.
SOURCE: AGSI, BNP PARIBAS

for a changing
world
At national level, it is worth highlighting the price cap mechanism for
gas purchased by power stations, which the Italian government is set
to introduce. In 2025, gas set the price of electricity in Italy in 73% of
cases (compared with an average of 40% across Europe). This price cap
mechanism, similar to that introduced in Spain in 2022, is, in principle,

only sustainable in the short term. Indeed, its cost is likely to be signi-
ficant given the Italian electricity mix’s high dependence on gas.
*****
Increased market dependence and the consequences of rising climate
risk for European electricity generation are altering the vulnerabilities
associated with the continent’s gas market. The sources of risk are
increasing in number and becoming harder to manage. This calls for
the continuation and strengthening of decarbonisation efforts in the

European policies launched in 2022.

美

企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
經濟 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數初值由 58.4 降至 57.6，企業活動仍強勁擴
張，內需及出口帶動訂單與就業成長；惟油價、關稅及供應鏈瓶頸推升
成本，售價漲幅創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨壓力持續升溫，後續須
關注聯準會政策動向。 
⚫ 9 月美國住宅市場續顯疲弱，建築許可及新屋開工分別年減 3.0%及
7.4%，反映住宅投資動能放緩。儘管多戶住宅許可與完工數回升，但整
體營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率、住房負擔能力及營建成本
對市場復甦的影響。 
⚫ 密西根大學公布之 10 月美國消費者信心指數降至 48.0，月減 0.2%、年
減 10.4%，顯示民眾對目前經濟環境仍缺乏信心，雖然對未來景氣的看
法略有改善，但高物價與借貸成本仍讓消費者感到壓力。此外，未來一
年及長期通膨預期分別升至 4.7%及 3.5%，反映民眾仍擔心物價持續上
漲。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 10 月 23 日川普宣布調整加拿大商品 50%關稅清單，10 月 30 日生效，
豁免部分紙製品、機械及包裝材料，並新增地毯、油漆等課稅項目。外
媒認為此舉與期中選舉有關，有助降低部分企業成本，但美加貿易摩擦
仍未化解。 
短評 
⚫ 10 月美國綜合產出指數雖略為下滑，但仍維持高檔，顯示內需與就業持
續支撐企業活動擴張。然而，消費者信心仍處低檔，反映高物價與借貸
成本持續壓抑購買意願，儘管民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但新屋
開工及建築許可同步下滑，顯示消費與住宅投資前景仍面臨挑戰。另一
方面，美國調整部分加拿大商品關稅，雖有助減輕部分企業進口成本，
但雙邊貿易摩擦尚未化解，政策不確定性仍存。

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.10.27 
一、 美國—企業活動維持擴張，消費信心與房市疲弱，通膨及關稅風險仍存 
標普全球(S&P Global)公布，2026 年 10 月美國綜合 PMI 產出指數(Composite 
PMI Output Index)初值由上月的 58.4 降至 57.6，其中服務業採購經理人指數
(Services PMI)由 58.8 降至 57.9，製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)則由
55.9 微升至 56.0，創 2022 年 5 月以來新高，企業活動雖略有放緩，但仍維持強
勁擴張。需求方面，內需穩健及出口改善帶動整體新訂單增速創逾 4 年半新高，
商品出口訂單亦為 2025 年 6 月以來首次增加；企業持續擴增人力，整體就業增
速為 2022 年 6 月以來次高。另一方面，未完成訂單增加、供應商交貨延遲加劇，
加上油價上漲及關稅推升能源、運輸與原物料成本，使企業投入成本維持高檔，
帶動售價漲幅升至 2022 年 8 月以來最高。儘管企業對未來一年營運前景的樂觀
程度略降，但仍處於今年相對高檔。整體而言，美國經濟在內外需及就業支撐下
維持擴張，惟供應鏈瓶頸與成本上升加劇通膨壓力，後續仍須關注物價走勢及聯
準會貨幣政策動向。 
密西根大學公布，2026 年 10 月美國消費者信心指數終值降至 48.0，略低於
上月的 48.1，月減 0.2%、年減 10.4%，並較今年 2 月下降約 15%，顯示消費者信
心持續處於低檔。分項來看，當前經濟狀況指數由 50.9 降至 46.7，月減 8.3%、
年減 20.3%；消費者預期指數則由 46.3 回升至 48.8，月增 5.4%、年減 3.0%，反
映民眾對未來景氣的看法略有改善，但整體信心仍偏弱。調查指出，高利率及購
買負擔壓力使耐久財購買條件下降 18%，拖累當前經濟狀況指數；未來一年企業
景氣預期雖改善約 14%，但仍低於去年同期 16%。通膨預期方面，未來一年通膨
預期由 4.6%升至 4.7%，長期通膨預期亦由 3.4%升至 3.5%，雙雙連續第二個月攀
升，創今年 5 月以來最高水準。密西根大學指出，2025 年關稅政策衝擊及 2026
年伊朗衝突引發的能源價格上漲，使民眾通膨預期再度升溫，且長期預期已明顯
高於 2019 年及 2024 年水準。整體而言，10 月美國消費者信心大致持平，但高物
價與借貸成本仍壓抑購買意願，加上短期及長期通膨預期持續上升，可能增加物
價回穩的難度，後續仍須關注能源價格、家庭消費及通膨預期變化對聯準會貨幣
政策的影響。

2026 年 9 月美國住宅市場建築許可年化總數降至 140.0 萬戶，月減 0.2%、年
減 3.0%，其中單戶住宅建築許可降至 84.5 萬戶，月減 4.0%、年減 4.8%，惟 5 戶
以上多戶住宅建築許可增至 50.3 萬戶，月增 5.9%、年增 1.0%，顯示不同住宅類
型開發規劃表現分歧。新屋開工年化數降至 122.1 萬戶，月減 5.2%、年減 7.4%，
其中單戶住宅開工 85.9 萬戶，較去年同期仍增 2.6%，但多戶住宅開工降至 34.9
萬戶，年減 25.9%，拖累整體營建動能。施工方面，在建住宅維持 128.0 萬戶，與
上月持平但較去年減少 3.3%；完工年化數則回升至 119.2 萬戶，月增 2.2%，主要
受多戶住宅完工月增 14.9%帶動，惟整體仍較去年同期減少 14.0%。整體而言，
美國住宅市場呈現建築許可及新屋開工下滑、在建規模持平及完工略有回升的態
勢，反映住宅營建活動仍偏弱，後續仍須關注房貸利率走勢、住房負擔能力及營
建成本變化對住宅市場復甦的影響。 
美國總統川普 10 月 23 日依據《1930 年關稅法》第 338 條簽署公告，調整部
分加拿大商品加徵 50%關稅的適用範圍，並自 10 月 30 日起生效。此次調整取消
膠合板、部分紙製品、機械與冷凍設備、包裝用木箱及紙箱、新鮮與乾燥海藻等
商品的額外關稅，同時新增閥門、地毯、油漆、洗髮精、可可粉、木製棺材、空
調設備及獨木舟等課稅項目，呈現局部鬆綁與新增課稅並行的政策方向。白宮表
示，此舉旨在減輕加拿大貿易措施對美國企業造成的負擔，並維護國內產業利益。
由於緬因州農業及製造業高度依賴加拿大進口物資，共和黨參議員柯林斯(Susan 
Collins)積極向白宮爭取關稅豁免，外媒亦將此次調整與 11 月 3 日期中選舉連結。
整體而言，新措施有助降低部分美國企業的進口成本，但美加貿易摩擦尚未完全
化解，後續仍須關注雙邊談判及關稅政策對供應鏈的影響。 
二、歐元區—法國動盪短期內難降溫，料將使央行保持審慎觀望態度 
 歐元區 10 月綜合採購經理人指數(PMI)下降 1.3 點至 51.9，抵銷上月的強勁
增幅，但為連續五個月高於 50 的榮枯線，主要係受到法國大規模抗議活動的影
響，使法國綜合 PMI 下降 2.7 點至 48.4；此外，德國綜合 PMI 下降 1 點至 52.8，
亦反映近期通膨壓力增加，使民眾傾向保守消費。若以產業區分，歐元區服務業、
製造業 PMI 分別降 1.3、0.5 點至 51.7、52.9；惟德國製造業 PMI 上升至相當強勁
的 54.2，反映德國政府擴張軍備投資以及基礎建設之需求，持續推動製造業生產
動能。

用相關內容。

6

法國抗爭活動規模逐漸擴大，各大工會與抗議團體已宣布在 10 月 31 日再度
發起大規模遊行，並預告於 11 月 5 日舉行全國性大罷工，抗議主要訴求包括改
善公共服務品質及凍結物價上漲。然而，在財政壓力巨大且國會缺乏穩定執政多
數的情況下，法國政府既無法強力通過重大的財政改革，也難以快速推出安撫反
對派的妥協法案或者公共支出。故部分較為悲觀的機構認為此波動盪可能會延續
至 2027 年 4 月的法國總統大選。受法國局勢影響，近期歐元區各國殖利率普遍
呈現上升趨勢，並對景氣產生暫時性的抑制效果。在此背景下，市場預期歐洲央
行在 10 月 28 日的政策會議將採取觀望立場，後續關注歐洲央行是否將透過政策
訊號或流動性工具，為平息法國主權債務利率而採取行動。 
三、日本—貿易逆差及通膨壓力延續，內需仍具韌性 
 9 月出口雖維持強勁，受半導體製造設備及汽車出口增加所帶動，出口額年增
16.7%；惟進口增幅更為顯著，9 月進口額年增 18.9%，其中原油及液化天然氣進
口額分別大增 51.1%及 39.2%，主要反映中東局勢推升能源價格，以及日本尋求
替代能源供應來源的影響。由於進口額高於出口額，9 月貿易逆差達 5,365 億日
圓，為連續第 5 個月呈現逆差；累計 2026 上半財年（4 至 9 月）貿易逆差亦較上
年同期擴大 98.8%至 2.82 兆日圓。整體而言，出口並未明顯轉弱，近期貿易逆差
擴大主要反映能源進口成本攀升，使進口增幅持續高於出口。 
 9 月全國消費者物價指數(CPI)年增 2.1%，即使政府透過能源及教育補貼等措
施抑制物價，通膨仍高於 2%，顯示實際通膨動能更為強勁。從物價結構來看，石
油、原物料及工資成本上升已逐步傳導至終端價格，漲價範圍由家具、醫療用品
及休閒用品等商品，進一步擴散至房屋維護、公共運輸、住宿及外食等服務項目，
顯示企業成本轉嫁範圍逐步擴大，通膨壓力亦由商品向服務領域擴散。隨通膨持
續高於 2%且漲價範圍擴大，亦為日本央行進一步推動貨幣政策正常化提供支撐。 
 10 月綜合 PMI 持平於 52.3，顯示經濟仍維持穩定擴張，其中製造業 PMI 下
降 0.8 點至 53.3，產出指數亦降至 3 月以來最低，主要反映新訂單及出口訂單成
長放緩，加上稀土金屬等原料供應受限，對生產形成一定壓力，惟生產與訂單仍
處擴張區間，尚未顯示製造業景氣明顯轉弱；服務業 PMI 回升 0.7 點至 52.0，新
業務及出口訂單同步改善，顯示服務需求仍具韌性，為支撐景氣的重要動能。另
一方面，能源、薪資及原物料成本居高不下，使投入價格壓力持續偏高，並逐漸

影響企業對未來景氣的信心。 
四、中國—第三季經濟成長率回升，但前景仍較為不利 
2026 年第三季 GDP 年增率初估值為 4.5%，較第二季的 4.3%上揚 0.2 個百分
點；累計 1-9 月 GDP 年增率則為 4.6%。第三季的二、三級產業年增率分別較第
二季上揚 0.4、0.1 個百分點至 3.4%，其中製造業、批發零售業年增率分別達 5.9%、
2.3%為主要動能，抵銷建築業年減 4.4%等拖累因素影響。整體而言，第三季 GDP
年增率上揚有部分受到中秋國慶連假落在 9 月末段(去年同期則於 10 月 1 日開
始)，致基期效果發揮部分影響，相關消費動能仍可能會在 10 月出現暫時性的回
落。 
在需求指標方面，(1)1-9 月累計零售額年增率為 1.1%，持平於 1-8 月的累計
年增率。其中 9 月零售額年增率增加 0.7 個百分點至 1.1%，部分受到新手機上市
帶動買氣，9 月通訊器材消費年增率高達 31.1%，加上中秋國慶長假帶動食品消
費年增率增加 1 個百分點至 2.1%所致；(2)1-9 月固定資產投資年減幅維持於 7.2%，
反映供應鏈移轉、政府主導去化過剩產能等因素導致民間投資需求急遽萎縮；萎
縮產業包含化工、醫藥、專用設備、汽車製造業、金屬製品業等；(3)1-9 月房地
產開發投資額年減幅由 19.9%擴大至 20.6%、施工面積年減幅為 12.8%，新建商
品房銷售面積年減幅擴大為 13.2%，房市投資、買氣皆持續低迷。 
受到出口維持高度成長帶動，9 月工業生產年增率較 8 月增加 0.3 個百分點
至 5.5%(累計 1-9 月工業生產年增率持平 1-8 月水準為 5.3%)。依產業別來看，9
月主要受到煤礦開採、化工、化纖等產業擴張影響，主因中秋與國慶長假前發電
業充實庫存水位，促使煤炭企業加快復產，加上油價上漲帶動相關中下游供應鏈
增產；惟高科技產品包含 IC、機器人、新能源車等年增率皆有放慢，反映去年中
秋國慶連假皆落在 10 月，使得去年 9 月工作日較多，致基期相對墊高的影響。 
展望未來，儘管政府 10 月初宣布釋放 5,500 億元人民幣的地方政府債務結存
限額，但額度下達到資金撥付往往需要數月時間，預期對於第四季的經濟影響有
限，使今年全年經濟成長率預估將落在 4.5%附近。此外，受歐盟右派勢力崛起及
保護主義影響，歐盟對中國貿易政策可能持續趨嚴，後續仍須關注雙方談判及溝
通結果，未來若外需動能減弱，預期中國政府將持續透過發展特定高科技產業及
扶植內需來支持經濟成長動能，以減輕房市不振及貿易環境惡化的衝擊。

五、印度及東南亞—新、馬出口保持強勁增速，菲律賓央行大舉升息 
(1)印度: 10 月 HSBC 綜合採購經理人指數（PMI）初值上升 0.8 點至 56.7，其中，
製造業 PMI 略下滑 0.2 點至 54.9、服務業 PMI 則上揚 1.0 點至 56.2，皆保持穩健
擴張態勢。由於美國、英國、中東及孟加拉等地的訂單增加，帶動新訂單及出口
訂單加速成長，製造商採購庫存增速創八個月來最快、成品庫存增速亦創歷史新
高，然而聘僱指數降至今年以來最低、未完成訂單下降，反映現階段人力充足。
另一方面，投入成本漲幅連續第七個月下降並降至近一年低點，產品售價漲幅亦
明顯放緩，通膨壓力緩解，有助提升企業獲利能力及消費者購買力，並且給予印
度央行（RBI）貨幣政策空間。 
(2)新加坡：9 月非石油國內出口（NODX）年增 68.4%，增速史上最快，連續第
23 個月正成長，其中，人工智慧需求強勁，帶動電子產品出口躍增 107.6%；非電
子產品出口亦大幅成長 54.2%，主要由船舶和遊艇組件所帶動。按出口目的地劃
分，對香港出口激增 121.1%、對台灣、韓國出口增幅達 96%，對美出口成長 73.2%，
但對印度出口下降 5.6%。展望未來，新加坡金融管理局在 10 月的宏觀經濟評估
報告中表示，直至 2027 年人工智慧 (AI) 驅動的成長將保持相對穩定，支持未
來幾季經濟成長表現。由於基數較高以及人工智慧相關投資預計放緩，全球利率
上升可能會對新加坡主要貿易夥伴的消費和非人工智慧投資造成壓力，可能會影
響外部需求，薪資成長放緩、招聘謹慎以及運輸成本上升可能會在 2026 年下半
年對可自由支配支出和零售活動構成壓力，預計 2027 年 GDP 成長將放緩。 
(3)印尼：印尼央行（BI）10 月會議連續第四次會議維持基準利率(7 天期逆回購利
率,BI-Rate)於 5.75%不變，貸款便利利率（LFR）維持在 6.50%不變，但將隔夜存
款便利利率（DFR）調高 25 個基點至 5%，旨在提高印尼盾資產的回報率，藉此
吸引並留住外資，同時宣布分兩階段調降存款準備率，11 月 1 日起下調 1 個百分
點至 8%，12 月 1 日起進一步降至 7%，以提供銀行體系流動性。整體而言，印尼
央行爲捍衛印尼盾，在 5 至 6 月間共升息 100 個基點，同時調降外資避險成本，
並採取「資金停留愈久、優惠愈高」的機制吸引外資流入。惟近期國際指數編纂
公司 MSCI 擬將印尼股市由”新興市場”降級為”獨立市場”，引發資金外流疑慮，
央行選擇利率按兵不動，搭配降準提供銀行體系流動性，以兼顧印尼盾穩定與國
內經濟需求，未來 MSCI 檢討進展(最快 2027 年 4 月公布)及印尼盾走勢，將持續
牽動央行後續政策。

(4)菲律賓：菲律賓央行（BSP）10 月 22 日宣布大幅調升基準利率(逆回購利率)2
碼至 5.50%，為 2023 年以來最大幅度升息。央行指出， 9 月通膨率已升至 7.2%，
遠高於 2%-4%的目標區間；同時核心通膨持續攀升，顯示價格上漲已由食品與能
源擴散至整體經濟。BSP 亦擔憂中東情勢推升國際油價、運輸費率調漲，以及聖
嬰現象衝擊農業生產，進一步推高物價。此外，披索貶至歷史低點附近，增加進
口通膨風險，因此採取較激進的緊縮措施以穩定市場預期，貨幣政策重點已由支
持經濟成長轉向優先遏制通膨風險。 
(5)馬來西亞：9 月出口額年增 54.5%至 MYR2,151 億元，其中電子&電氣產品出
口大增 69.2%，持續反映 AI 帶動的強勁需求，礦產品出口亦大幅成長 56.3%，抵
銷農產品出口續跌 5.7%的影響，對台灣、美國出口大增 175.2%、89.9%、對新加
坡出口增幅亦達 50.4%。9 月進口額成長 52.7%至 MYR1,814 億元，受惠出口引
申需求，中間產品、資本貨物進口分別成長 69.6%、20.9%，消費品進口轉為正成
長 18.2%，自中國和新加坡進口分別成長 67.6%、90.7%。進出口相抵，9 月貿易
順差達 MYR337 億元、年增 64.9%。 
六、台灣—外銷訂單創新高，產業景氣冷熱不均 
9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，再創單月新高，年增 62.4%，主要受惠於人
工智慧、高效能運算與雲端服務等應用蓬勃發展，以及消費性電子新品陸續推出，
帶動相關供應鏈維持高檔；加上國際原物料價格上漲，推升整體接單表現。惟各
產業接單動能仍呈分化，科技產品需求強勁，傳統產業景氣則相對疲弱。經濟部
指出，雖有年底節慶補貨潮挹注，但傳統產業部分產能過剩，終端需求仍未見明
顯回升。

四、前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元
10月末，本外币存款余额358.81万亿元，同比增长7.8%。月末人民币存款余额350.65万亿元，同比增长7.7%。前十个月人民币存款增加21.97万亿元。其中，住户存款增加8.47万亿元，非金融企业存款增加1.91万亿元，财政性存款增加3.03万亿元，非银行业金融机构存款
增加5.99万亿元。10月末，外币存款余额1.22万亿美元，同比增长16.9%。前十个月外币存款增加1553亿美元。
五、前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元
10月末，本外币贷款余额287.25万亿元，同比增长4.6%。月末人民币贷款余额283.33万亿元，同比增长4.7%。前十个月人民币贷款增加11.42万亿元。分部门看，住户贷款减少1.46万亿元，其中，短期贷款减少1.41万亿元，中长期贷款减少501亿元；企（事）业单位贷款
增加12.39万亿元，其中，短期贷款增加4.65万亿元，中长期贷款增加6.69万亿元，票据融资增加9283亿元；非银行业金融机构贷款减少2395亿元。10月末，外币贷款余额5831亿美元，同比增长5.2%。前十个月外币贷款增加381亿美元。
六、10月份银行间人民币市场同业拆借月加权平均利率为1.3%，质押式债券回购月加权平均利率为1.33%
10月份银行间人民币市场以拆借、现券和回购方式合计成交167.98万亿元，日均成交9.33万亿元，日均成交同比增长1.9%。其中，同业拆借日均成交同比下降10.8%，现券日均成交同比增长4.7%，质押式回购日均成交同比增长1.3%。10月份同业拆借加权平均利率为1.3%，
比上月低0.1个百分点，比上年同期低0.09个百分点；质押式回购加权平均利率为1.33%，比上月低0.07个百分点，比上年同期低0.07个百分点。
七、10月份经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元
10月份，经常项下跨境人民币结算金额为1.9万亿元，其中货物贸易、服务贸易及其他经常项目分别为1.43万亿元、0.47万亿元；直接投资跨境人民币结算金额为0.56万亿元，其中对外直接投资、外商直接投资分别为0.21万亿元、0.35万亿元。

2026年10月金融数据

2026年11月13日，人民银行发布2026年1-10月金融统计数据报告。数据显示，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长
4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。
一、社会融资规模存量同比增长7.2%
初步统计，2026年10月末社会融资规模存量为469.16万亿元，同比增长7.2%。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款余额279.31万亿元，同比增长4.6%；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币余额1.26万亿元，同比增长9%；委托贷款余额11.53万亿元，同比增长1.7%；信托
贷款余额4.49万亿元，同比下降0.6%；未贴现的银行承兑汇票余额2.05万亿元，同比下降4.4%；企业债券余额36.92万亿元，同比增长9.6%；政府债券余额106.41万亿元，同比增长14.4%；非金融企业境内股票余额12.77万亿元，同比增长5.5%。
二、前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元
初步统计，2026年前十个月社会融资规模增量累计为28.61万亿元，比上年同期少2.29万亿元。其中，对实体经济发放的人民币贷款增加10.91万亿元，同比少增3.61万亿元；对实体经济发放的外币贷款折合人民币增加2546亿元，同比多增3692亿元；委托贷款增加2084亿
元，同比多增1002亿元；信托贷款减少1798亿元，同比多减3958亿元；未贴现的银行承兑汇票减少939亿元，同比多减1055亿元；企业债券净融资3.1万亿元，同比多1.27万亿元；政府债券净融资11.49万亿元，同比少4592亿元；非金融企业境内股票融资5705亿元，同比多
1842亿元。
三、广义货币增长7.1%
10月末，广义货币(M2)余额359.06万亿元，同比增长7.1%。狭义货币(M1)余额116.9万亿元，同比增长4.4%。流通中货币(M0)余额15万亿元，同比增长10.7%。前十个月净投放现金9023亿元。

Document 105: 2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し ～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026年11月 17日

1/3 
2027年・春闘賃上げ率の見通し 
～賃上げ率は４年連続の５％台か～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

（要旨） 
○ 2027年の春闘賃上げ率を、厚生労働省ベースで 5.15％（2026年：5.18％）、連合ベースで
4.98％（同 5.01％）と予測する。2027年春闘でもほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、厚生労
働省ベースでは４年連続で５％台が実現するだろう。連合は 2027 年春闘の要求目安を前年
よりやや引き上げ、経団連も高い賃上げの継続に前向きな姿勢を維持している。ＵＡゼンセ
ンも前年並みの高い要求水準を示すなど、労使双方の賃上げ姿勢にトーンダウンはみられな
い。 
○ 構造的な人手不足が続くなか、人材の確保・定着に向けた賃上げの必要性は高い。企業収益
も総じて堅調であり、高水準の利益が賃上げ余力を支えている。2026年の物価上昇率が前年
から鈍化することは賃上げ率の下押し要因となる。一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初に
は物価上昇率が再び高まる可能性があり、実質賃金の安定的な改善を求める労使双方の問題
意識も強い。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続
する環境は維持されている。 
○ ４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、日本銀行にとっても金融政策の正常化に向けて前
向きな材料となる。特に、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを前提として賃
金を設定するようになっているかどうかが、２％の物価安定目標の定着を見極めるうえで重
要となる。

2027年春闘でも前年並みの高い賃上げ率を予想 
2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 5.15％と予測する（厚生労働省「民間主要企業春季賃上げ要求・妥結
状況」ベース）。2024年春闘では5.33％、2025年では 5.52％、2026年でも 5.18％と３年連続で
５％台の高い賃上げが実現したが、2027年もほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げが続き、４年連続で５％
台となるだろう。 
春闘においては、厚生労働省よりも公表時期が早い、連合による集計値が注目されることが多
い。この連合ベースの数字では、2027年の春闘賃上げ率を 4.98％と予測する（2026年春闘：
5.01％）。５％台に乗るかどうかは微妙なところだが、こちらも歴史的にみれば高い賃上げとなる

### Page 2

2/3 
だろう。

組合側、経営側ともトーンダウンはみられず  
組合側、経営側とも賃上げに前向きな姿勢を維持している。連合は 10月22日に公表した2027
年春闘基本構想において、定昇込みの賃上げ要求を「６％中心、５％以上」、ベースアップ等の賃
上げ分を「４％中心、３％以上」とし、前年の賃上げ「５％以上」、ベア「３％以上」よりも要求
姿勢をやや強めた。経営側も賃上げの継続に前向きだ。経団連の経営労働政策委員会報告原案で
は、高い賃上げの「社会全体への浸透」が掲げられていると報じられた。また、筒井経団連会長
は 11月９日の記者会見で、ベア実施の検討を賃金交渉のスタンダードとする方針を堅持する考え
を示した。また、主要産業別労働組合組織では、ＵＡゼンセンが定昇込み６％、ベア等４％とい
う前年と同等の高い要求方針素案を示している。中東情勢悪化によるコスト上昇という逆風があ
るなかでも、労使ともに賃上げ姿勢がトーンダウンしている様子は窺えない。もっとも、連合の
要求目安は強まったものの、ＵＡゼンセンの要求率は前年と同水準であり、現時点では賃上げ率
のさらなる加速を見込むほどの材料が揃っているわけではないことには注意が必要だ。

物価は前年から鈍化も、人手不足と好調な企業収益が押し上げ要因に 
高い賃上げを支える最大の要因は、構造的な人手不足である。生産年齢人口の減少が続くな
か、企業の人手不足感は依然として強く、人材の確保・定着は重要な経営課題となっている。特
に若年層を中心に採用競争が厳しくなっており、既存人材のつなぎ止めのためにも企業は継続的
な賃上げを行う必要がある。ここ数年の春闘では、こうした人手不足を背景に高い賃上げが実現
しており、企業の賃金設定行動にも変化が生じている。2027年春闘でも人手不足を背景とした賃
上げ圧力は強いと考えられる。 
好調な企業収益も、高い賃上げを支える材料になる。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇は企業収
益の下押し要因となっているものの、価格転嫁の進展やＡＩ関連需要の拡大などを背景に、企業
収益は総じて堅調に推移しており、水準も非常に高い。企業規模や業種によって差はあるもの
の、企業収益が全体として高水準にあり、賃上げ余力は十分に確保されていると見てよい。 
一方、物価については、2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍化することが賃上げ率の下押し要因
になるとみられる。春闘交渉では前年の暦年の物価上昇率が参照されることが多いが、2026年の
上昇率（ＣＰＩ総合）は前年比＋２％前後と、2025年の前年比＋3.2％から鈍化する見込みであ
り、物価高への対応という観点からの賃上げ圧力は弱まる可能性がある。 
もっとも、暦年でみれば物価上昇率は鈍化する一方、春闘交渉が本格化する 2027年初には再び
高まり、ＣＰＩは前年比＋３％台に達する可能性がある。この場合、実質賃金が再びマイナスに
転じる恐れもある。そもそも、過去数年にわたって実質賃金が減少してきたことで、家計には賃
上げの恩恵を十分に実感できないという不満が残っている。実質賃金の改善の遅れへの問題意識
は組合側、経営側にも共有されており、実質賃金を安定的に増加させることは 2027年春闘でも重
要な課題となるだろう。春闘交渉の時点で物価上昇率の再加速が意識されれば、春闘賃上げ率の

### Page 3

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
3/3 
上振れ要因となる可能性もあることには注意したい。

２％「定着」判断の重要な材料に  
以上を踏まえ、2027年春闘では5.15％と、ほぼ前年並みの高い賃上げを予想する（厚生労働省
ベース）。賃上げ率が一段と加速することまでは見込みにくいものの、高い賃上げを継続する環境
は維持されていると考えられる。 
４年連続で５％台の賃上げが実現すれば、賃金上昇の持続性を重視する日本銀行にとっても前
向きな材料となるだろう。特に重要なのは、労使双方が今後も２％程度の物価上昇が続くことを
前提として賃金を設定するようになっているかどうかである。2026年の物価上昇率が前年から鈍
化するなかでも高い賃上げが維持されれば、こうした賃金設定が広がりつつあることを示す一つ
の材料となる。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が２％の物価安定目標とおおむね整合的な水準に達し
たとの認識を示したが、２％の物価安定目標が「定着」したとまでは判断していない。2027年春
闘は、その定着を見極めるうえでも重要な判断材料となるだろう。

みられず～ | 新家 義貴 | 第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所 
2027年春闘の初動モメンタム・アップデート ～経労委報告原案と産別要求からも賃上げ意欲を確

Valuation Methodology and Risks
                        MPI Corporation (6223.TWO)                    
We derive our price target from a residual income model, as we believe this can best capture 
the long-term value. We assume a 9.78% cost of equity (risk-free rate 2.0% and risk premium 
6.0%), a payout ratio of 67%, a medium-term growth rate of 16.0%, and a terminal growth rate 
of 4.0%. Our price target implies 50x/25x 2027e/2028e P/E. We believe its valuation will con-
tinue to climb along with growing earnings.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

### Page 3

Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
3
CPO: New growth opportunities from vertical integration — Hon Hai sees
significant long-term opportunities in both ASIC and vertical integration including
optical interconnect technologies. In optical networking, management believes the
transition from electrical to optical interconnects is structurally inevitable as AI
models expand and memory-bandwidth requirements increase. Hon Hai’s CPO

optical switching already starts mass volume production for its major customer,
and its micron-level precision manufacturing expertise in optical alignment,
connectors, and electrical-to-optical conversion could create additional value
through component integration and higher system-level content. Its extensive
engineering resources, ODM/JDM development capabilities, and vertically
integrated manufacturing platform should strengthen its competitive positioning.

Management expressed strong confidence in optical growth opportunities but did
not provide specific 2027 revenue guidance.

© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research Estimates
Robust financials and earnings growth, maintain Buy — Thanks to strong
demand from major CSP clients and its continuous automation and vertical
integration development, Hon Hai’s capex increased 43% YTD. Nevertheless, its
EBITDA increased 50.3% YoY to NT$369.2bn. To improve capital efficiency, Hon

Hai plans to expand consignment arrangements, optimize inventory and
receivables management, and carefully evaluate investment payback periods.
Management reiterated its long-term operating margin floor of above 3%, with
further potential upside from scale economies, automation, product mix
improvements, and greater vertical integration.
Given Hon Hai’s solid result and better operational leverage, we raise our
2026/2027/2028 EPS by 4%/5%/5%. Considering its solid execution, stable

dividend yield and attractive valuation, we reiterate our Buy rating with TP NT$400
unchanged. Our TP implies 15x of our 2027 EPS projection.

### Page 9

Hon Hai Precision (2317.TW)
12 November 2026 Citi Research
9
Hon Hai Precision
Company description
Established in 1974, Hon Hai has become the largest EMS player in the world
in terms of sales. Through its vertical integration model or "eCMMS" (e-
enabled Components, Modules, Moves and Services), it provides
components, modules and system assembly products to many global OEMs
in the PC, communication and consumer sectors.
Investment strategy
We rate Hon Hai shares as Buy as we believe the company’s margins will

continue to improve, backed by a renewed focus on profitability, operational
efficiency and new businesses development. We think there’s more upside to
go from current level, as the underlying drivers are not just about product
cycle, but also about fundamental transformation and new business
opportunities.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$400 is based on 15x average 2026E-27E EPS, which is
at the higher end of the historical trading range. We believe this multiple is

justified by the company’s enhanced sales growth and profit margin outlook,
as well as new endeavors into AI server, EV, digital health and robotics. Hon
Hai has been able to generate positive earnings streams, and thus we believe
a P/E valuation methodology is appropriate.
Risks
Downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) slower-than-expected iPhone demand; 2) intensified peer
competition; 3) weaker-than-expected execution on new business progress;

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds

### Page 2

Investment thesis and Valuation & Risks

Investment Thesis: WNC 
WNC is a leading network communications solutions provider with technology across 
WiFi, Cellular, and Satellite. The company is the leading original design manufacturer 
(ODM) for major networking brands (e.g. Cisco, HP, etc.) and serves the leading US 
telecom operators (e.g. Verizon, AT&T, etc.). WNC is also the sole supplier of Starlink’s 
low earth orbits (LEO) satellites’ user terminal (power supply, antenna and router). We 
see WNC as an early beneficiary in the growing Space Economy, riding on Starlink’s 
subscribers growth, along with recovering traditional networking business driven by a 
low base in 2025, WiFi 7, 5G FWA (fixed wireless access) investments from US and 
Europe, and customers’ needs on diversifying suppliers, leading to a 14% NI CAGR in 
2026-28E. WNC is trading at 15x 2027E P/E, lower than the average of its main peers, 
which is attractive in our view. We are Buy rated on WNC. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology: WNC 
Valuation methodology: We use a near-term P/E to derive our 12m target price for 
WNC, consistent with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our TP of NT$380.0 on 
a target P/E multiple of 28.0x on a forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived 
from the correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers. 
Key downside risks: fiercer-than-expected competition in satellite communication, 
slower-than-expected Wi-Fi 7 or 5G FWA ramp-up in US and Europe markets.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(YoY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(YoY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(YoY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(YoY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(YoY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(YoY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.”2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.

While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings

(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.

Bank of America, Merrill, their affiliates and advisors do not provide legal, tax or accounting advice. Clients should consult their legal and/or tax advisors before making any financial decisions.
This material does not take into account a client’s particular investment objectives, financial situations, or needs and is not intended as a recommendation, offer, or solicitation for the purchase
or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and

of America, Merrill or any affiliate to sell or provide, or a specific invitation for a consumer to apply for, any particular retail financial product or service that may be available.
The Chief Investment Office (“CIO”) provides thought leadership on wealth management, investment strategy and global markets; portfolio management solutions; due diligence; and solutions
oversight and data analytics. CIO viewpoints are developed for Bank of America Private Bank, a division of Bank of America, N.A., (“Bank of America”) and Merrill Lynch, Pierce, Fenner & Smith

The Global Wealth & Investment Management Investment Strategy Committee (“GWIM ISC”) is responsible for developing and coordinating recommendations for short-term and long-term
investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.
BofA Global Research is research produced by BofA Securities, Inc. (“BofAS”) and/or one or more of its affiliates. BofAS is a registered broker-dealer, Member SIPC and wholly owned subsidiary

of Bank of America Corporation.
All recommendations must be considered in the context of an individual investor’s goals, time horizon, liquidity needs and risk tolerance. Not all recommendations will be in the best interest
of all investors.
Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

.

.

.

.

Document 342: FX Daily_ Commodity complex is having a moment

Source PDF: all report pdf/FX Daily_ Commodity complex is having a moment.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

The dollar is bid across the board as a strong US economy and
above-target inﬂation keeps expectations of another 75bp in
Fed tightening alive. At the same time, commodity
currencies are starting to underperform as industrial metals
and energy take a leg lower. Investors are yet to lean into
another decent leg higher in the dollar, but that is the risk

Chris Turner, Francesco Pesole and Frantisek Taborsky
Domestic politics, dollar strength and falling commodities weigh on the
Brazilian real
Please see our 2027 FX Outlook for all the key themes driving
FX trends next year.
USD: Topside pressure building
We have been discussing it for more than a week now, but those
comments from the Fed's Chris Waller last Tuesday really focused
minds on an extended Fed tightening cycle. Since then, price data
has come in hot, while US activity data is also performing well. More

### Page 2

insights into the US economy will come from tonight's release of
the Fed's Beige Book ahead of the next FOMC meeting on 9
December. So far, the 15% correction in the Nasdaq has yet to really
dent those Fed tightening expectations. And tech stocks might
receive some support today from Nvidia's strong third-quarter
earnings release last night and today's Anthropic's $90bn IPO set
to price towards the high end of the $70-78 per share range.
Aside from the strong US economy and the Fed story, we are also

seeing some underperformance of commodity currencies. Here,
industrial metals are under pressure – especially iron ore – and even
copper is starting to turn lower. Our Commodity Strategist, Ewa
Manthey, discusses the prospect for industrial metals in the Latam
section of our 2027 FX Outlook. There seem to be both demand and
supply trends at work here, but ongoing pressure on industrial
metals usually spells trouble for the Australian dollar, the South

African rand and adds to the already weakened position of the
Brazilian real. With central banks looking to slow, not accelerate,
aggregate demand, it looks as though these trends could have a
little further to run.
Price action suggests investors, already long the dollar, are not yet
ready to chase the dollar much higher. But it is hard to stand in the
way of a DXY advance to the next resistance area around 104.60.
US housing data today looks unlikely to move markets.

Chris Turner
EUR: Dollar story dominates
Despite some seemingly better news out of France yesterday,
EUR/USD continues to trade below 1.10 as the dollar story
dominates. The FX options market suggests investors are yet to
lean into much further EUR/USD downside from here. For example,
one-month EUR/USD traded volatility has not moved much, while
the price of a one-month risk reversal – the price of a euro put over
a euro call option – remains steady near 0.5%. That probably

reﬂects a market already short EUR/USD and covering, rather than
opening new positions below 1.10.

### Page 3

However, the US story is looking increasingly solid and the balance
of risks favours this EUR/USD move extending to the 1.0880 area. In
focus today is the ﬁnal release of the eurozone October CPI
(expected at 4.0% year-on-year headline and 2.5% core) and a few
ECB speakers later in the day.
Chris Turner
GBP: Further relative strength
EUR/GBP has corrected a little higher this European morning as the
latest services inﬂation data for October came in slightly below

expectations at 3.7% year-on-year. We doubt that is enough to
trigger a wholesale re-pricing of the UK money market curve,
although lower energy prices may be playing a greater role here
today.
In our 2027 FX Outlook, we have a 0.83 forecast for EUR/GBP at the
end of this year. A more hawkish Bank of England relative to the ECB
over the coming months and PM Andy Burnham's eﬀorts to get
closer to Europe are two factors which should keep EUR/GBP oﬀered.

This pair does exhibit very low realised volatility, and therefore the
150bp pick-up in GBP over EUR deposit rates is meaningful support
for sterling.
Chris Turner
BRL: Waiting on a new ﬁnance minister
The Brazilian real remains fragile as domestic politics, a strong
dollar and high US rates, plus now lower commodity prices all
weigh. The real looks unlikely to receive much of a reprieve from the
external environment anytime soon, but President Lula's choice of a

ﬁnance minister will have a big say in whether the domestic story
exacerbates or reverses the recent heavy losses in the real. On
Monday, a report that the left-leaning Aloizio Mercadante was
being considered for the position hit the real.
Given all the concerns over the ﬁscal trajectory in Brazil, the choice
of ﬁnance minister will be a key signal to the markets of President
Lula's agenda. Were a name like Geraldo Alckmin to emerge as a

Document 425: US Economics Analyst_ 2027 US Outlook_ The End of the Inflation Shocks

Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 20
Status: ok

### Page 1

The main economic surprise of 2026, the energy shock from the Iran war, has not n
prevented the US economy from growing at a healthy pace but has reversed 
progress on inflation.  The key drivers of growth and inflation in 2026—the war, 
tax cuts, tariffs, and AI price pressures—should all have less impact next year.  
We expect this to leave 2027 GDP growth at an above-consensus 2.4% and the 
labor market stable, while allowing core PCE inflation to fall substantially to 2.4%. 
Growth should follow the same pattern in 2027 as in 2026: solid consumption n
growth and strong business investment growth offset by drags from housing and 
trade.  Consumption should grow a bit over 2%, driven mainly by firmer and more 
evenly balanced real income growth as inflation moderates and the boosts from 
last year’s tax cuts and wealth effects diminish.  AI drives half of our 8% forecast 
for business investment growth but contributes only modestly to measured GDP 
growth because much of the equipment is imported or undercounted in the GDP 
statistics.  Housing investment will likely contract again as poor affordability 
continues to depress demand. 
Near-potential growth should keep the labor market stable in 2027.  This year n
began with fears of a looming AI-driven layoff wave, but the layoff rate instead 
remained historically low, and the labor market settled into a slightly softer 
low-hire, low-fire form of full employment where wage growth has stabilized at a 
subdued pace of about 3.5%.  We expect this equilibrium to last for another year 
before AI displacement begins to have a larger impact in subsequent years. 
While the near-term inflation outlook remains uncertain, we are more confident n
that inflation will fall substantially by late 2027 as the effects of tariffs, the Iran 
war energy shock, and AI price pressures diminish.  We see these three forces as 
the key to the outlook because the economy is not overheated at a 
macroeconomic level or even at an industry level outside of pockets tied to the 
AI boom, and high inflation does not appear to have become entrenched in 
expectations.  As a result, inflation should moderate as long as these forces 
subside and no new shocks emerge. 
The Fed is likely to remain firmly focused on inflation in 2027.  We forecast two n
more 25bp rate hikes in December 2026 and March 2027 to a peak of 
4.25-4.5%, followed by gradual cuts back to 3.25-3.5% starting in November, the 
final meeting of 2027, once inflation has declined meaningfully.  The FOMC will 
also discuss the conclusions of the task forces next year, but we expect any

+1(212)357-2619 |

### Page 2

institutional changes to be limited.  In particular, a large reduction in the size of the
Fed’s balance sheet is unlikely, though a shift in the composition of its assets is
possible.
The most obvious risk to our forecast of sturdy growth and a long-awaited decline in n
inflation is that the inflation shocks of the last two years, which we and most
forecasters did not anticipate, could last longer, or new shocks could arise.  We also
see downside risks to growth from the war, a longer period of higher interest rates,

or earlier and more rapid job displacement from AI.  But our best guess is that there
will be fewer big policy-driven macroeconomic shocks in 2027.
